@@881586 <h> With chilling candour , a paper from a senior government official laid out the difficulties that Britain would face in the proposed Common Market . <p> All across Europe , from riots in Greece to those protest votes for Marine Le Pen and George Galloway , we see signs of how alienated people now feel from the " political class " which rules over our lives , out of touch with the rest of us , without meaningful opposition , no longer responsive to any democratic control . I am reminded of a document I discovered in the National Archives at Kew in January 2002 , when sifting through papers released under the 30-year rule relating to Britain 's negotiations to join the Common Market . It was a confidential 1971 memorandum , clearly written by a senior Foreign Office official , headed " Sovereignty and the Community " . <p> With chilling candour , this paper ( from FCO folder 30/1048 ) predicted that it would take 30 years for the British people to wake up to the real nature of the European project that Edward @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ time it would be too late for them to leave . Its author made clear that the Community was headed for economic , monetary and fiscal union , with a common foreign and defence policy , which would constitute the greatest surrender of Britain 's national sovereignty in history . Since " Community law " would take precedence over our own , ever more power would pass to this new bureaucratic system centred in Brussels -- and , as the role of Parliament diminished , this would lead to a " popular feeling of alienation from government " . <p> It would therefore become the duty of politicians " not to exacerbate public concern by attributing unpopular measures ... to the remote and unmanageable workings of the Community " . Politicians of all parties should be careful to conceal the fact that controversial laws originated in Brussels . By this means it might be possible to preserve the illusion that the British government was still sovereign , " for this century at least " -- by which time it would no longer be possible for us to leave . <p> In @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ our politicians should connive in concealing what Heath was letting us in for , not least in hiding the extent to which Britain would no longer be a democratic country but one essentially governed by unelected and unaccountable officials . <p> One way to create an illusion that this system was still democratic , this anonymous mandarin suggested , would be to give people the chance to vote for new representatives at European , regional and local levels . A few years later , we saw the creation of an elected European Parliament -- as we see today a craze for introducing elected mayors , as meaningless local figureheads . <p> But where the author was perhaps shrewder than he knew was in predicting how all this would eventually lead to " we the people " feeling alienated from the whole process of how we are governed . We now see a gulf yawning between , on the one hand , the consensus government of our new nomenklatura and , on the other , all the rest of us , aware that we are democratically powerless . To the growing groundswell @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ who rule us with such sublime incompetence will eventually find they have no answer . <p> ( Anyone wishing to read the relevant document may find it , with commentary , at www.eureferendum.com . ) <p> The green mystery we must ask our MPs to explain <p> David Cameron , reiterating his claim to lead " the greenest government ever " , has again highlighted one of the great political mysteries of our time -- one I would be grateful for my readers ' help in unravelling . Four years ago , our MPs voted almost unanimously for by far the most expensive law ever enacted by Parliament . According to the Department for Energy and Climate Change ( Decc ) , the Climate Change Act will cost us all up to ? 18 billion every year until 2050 . It commits Britain , uniquely in the world , to cut its emissions of carbon dioxide and other greenhouse gases to only a fifth of what they were in 1990 . <p> As we all know , emissions of CO2 from fossil fuels are inseparable from almost every economic activity of @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ per cent of the electricity needed to provide us with almost all the necessities of life . Without CO2 we would not be able to light and heat our homes , power our computers , get money from cashpoints , buy food from shops . Our transport system would be paralysed . <p> Hence the mystery : how can we meet that obligation to cut our emissions of CO2 by 80 per cent in just 40 years ? After six hours debating this in 2008 , 463 MPs of all parties voted for it , only three against . But of the 50 MPs who spoke , not one showed the slightest interest in how the target might be met . <p> May I therefore ask as many of my readers as possible to write to their local MPs , asking how we can hope to achieve that four-fifths cut without virtually closing down our economy ? <p> It wo n't be enough to reply that , by then , we shall be able to generate almost all our electricity from " renewables " . However many windmills and solar panels @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ sun , these could at best provide only a fraction of what is needed . Even if a few nuclear power stations somehow get built by then , they could be only a very partial answer . So how will our economy continue to function ? That is what we want our MPs to tell us . <p> In fact there is another mystery they need to explain . It seems , according to Decc 's figures , that we are already miraculously on course to meet our target . In 1990 , Britain supposedly emitted 590 million tons of CO2 . But by 2011 , according to Decc 's provisional figures , this had already been slashed to just 456 million , a drop of 23 per cent . The chief explanation Decc offers for this is the switch of many of our power stations , after 1991 , from coal to gas , which is less carbon-intensive . But even this by 2007 had only helped us to cut CO2 by 8 per cent . By far the biggest drop -- a further 15 per cent , so Decc @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ came into force and we went into recession . Nevertheless , Decc would have us believe that in two of those years , 2009 and 2011 , emissions fell by 50 and 40 million tons , respectively ( though in 2010 they rose by 24 million ) . <p> How can our MPs explain such a staggering reduction ? It may give the politically convenient impression that the Government is meeting its target but it is , frankly , quite implausible . <p> The MPs might also be reminded that Britain accounts for 1.6 per cent of the world 's man-made CO2 emissions . China 's emissions increase by that much each year , and now amount to 25 per cent of the global total . So , finally , can our MPs explain why we should close down most of our economy when this will have so little effect on the supposed global problem ? If readers would be good enough to send their MPs ' replies to me , c/o The Sunday Telegraph , I look forward to reporting them . 
@@882464 <h> Wind farms can cause climate change , according to new research , that shows for the first time the new technology is already pushing up temperatures . <p> Usually at night the air closer to the ground becomes colder when the sun goes down and the earth cools . <p> But on huge wind farms the motion of the turbines mixes the air higher in the atmosphere that is warmer , pushing up the overall temperature . <p> Satellite data over a large area in Texas , that is now covered by four of the world 's largest wind farms , found that over a decade the local temperature went up by almost 1C as more turbines are built . <p> This could have long term effects on wildlife living in the immediate areas of larger wind farms . <p> It could also affect regional weather patterns as warmer areas affect the formation of cloud and even wind speeds . <p> It is reported China is now erecting 36 wind turbines every day and Texas is the largest producer of wind power in the US . <p> @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ Atmospheric and Environmental Sciences at the University of New York , who led the study , said further research is needed into the affect of the new technology on the wider environment . <p> " Wind energy is among the world 's fastest growing sources of energy . The US wind industry has experienced a remarkably rapid expansion of capacity in recent years , " he said . " While converting wind 's kinetic energy into electricity , wind turbines modify surface-atmosphere exchanges and transfer of energy , momentum , mass and moisture within the atmosphere . These changes , if spatially large enough , might have noticeable impacts on local to regional weather and climate . " <p> The study , published in Nature , found a " significant warming trend " of up to 0.72C ( 1.37F ) per decade , particularly at night-time , over wind farms relative to near-by non-wind-farm regions . <p> The team studied satellite data showing land surface temperature in west-central Texas . <p> " The spatial pattern of the warming resembles the geographic distribution of wind turbines and the year-to-year land surface temperature @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ to 2011 , consistent with the increasing number of operational wind turbines with time , " said Prof Zhou . <p> However Prof Zhou pointed out the most extreme changes were just at night and the overall changes may be smaller . <p> Also , it is much smaller than the estimated change caused by other factors such as man made global warming . <p> " Overall , the warming effect reported in this study is local and is small compared to the strong background year-to-year land surface temperature changes , " he added . <p> The study read : " Despite debates regarding the possible impacts of wind farms on regional to global scale weather and climate , modelling studies agree that they can significantly affect local scale meteorology . " <p> Professor Steven Sherwood , co-Director of the Climate Change Research Centre at the University of New South Wales , said the research was ' pretty solid ' . <p> " This makes sense , since at night the ground becomes much cooler than the air just a few hundred meters above the surface , and the wind farms @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ mix together , thus the ground does n't get quite as cool . This same strategy is commonly used by fruit growers ( who fly helicopters over the orchards rather than windmills ) to combat early morning frosts . " 
@@882609 <h> The Building Regulations : An overview <p> Doug Basen , LABC Technical Policy Manager , gives an overview of the current Building Regulations , as well as outlining proposed changes over the next 12 months . <p> The Building Regulations are national regulations in force throughout England and Wales made under powers provided in the Building Act 1984 , and apply in England and Wales . The current edition of the regulations is ' The Building Regulations 2010 ' and the majority of building projects are required to comply with them . They exist to ensure the health and safety of people in and around all types of buildings ( i.e. domestic , commercial and industrial ) . They also provide for energy conservation , and for access and facilities to buildings to ensure they can be used by everyone . <p> The local authority has always administered the regulations , however since 1984 Building Control has been open to competition from the private sector and Approved Inspectors can now also provide the building control service . Only Local Authorities have powers to enforce the Regulations . @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ a fee . The important and fundamental difference between the two is local authorities are only permitted to charge a fee sufficient to cover the service they offer , whereas private bodies will invariably make a charge that includes an element of profit . <p> So if you want peace of mind for a comprehensive , independent and accountable service use your local authority building control provider . <h> What do the Regulations cover ? <p> The regulations cover the majority of building work that is undertaken and includes the erection of new buildings , the extension or alteration of existing buildings and the provision of a controlled service or fitting ( e.g. drainage , heat producing appliances ) . <p> The Regulations also apply where certain changes of use to a building occur , however there are certain minor building works that do not require approval . <p> The Regulations are arranged in a series of Approved Documents that provide guidance as to how you can comply with the various regulations . In general if you adopt any of the solutions in these documents there is a presumption the work @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ to adopt a solution that is not contained within the documents it will be for you to prove this particular solution does indeed meet the requirements of the regulations . <p> The Approved Documents are divided into a series of discreet areas covering the various elements of a building ; each document sets out by stating the requirements of the relevant regulation , and then offers a wide range of potential solutions , technical details and approaches as to how the requirements can be met . The approved documents are divided into the following generic headings ; <h> Part A <p> Structure requires buildings to be designed , constructed and altered so as to be structurally safe and robust , and also so as not to impair the structural stability of other buildings . It stipulates design standards that should be adopted for use on all buildings and additionally gives simple design rules for most masonry and timber elements for traditional domestic buildings . For buildings other than small residential , there are many references to codes and standards for structural design . The current Approved Document is the 2004 edition @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ <h> Part B <p> Fire safety covers all fire precautionary measures that are necessary to provide safety from fire that will safeguard building occupants , persons in the vicinity of buildings , and fire fighters . Requirements and guidance cover means of escape in case of fire ; fire detection and warning systems ; fire resistance of structural elements ; fire separation , protection , compartmentation and isolation to prevent fire spread and conflagration ; control of flammable materials ; and access and facilities for fire fighting . Changes to Part B were introduced on 6 April 2007 . The most significant change is the separation of the guidance into two documents , volume one covering dwelling houses and volume two for everything else . Fire safety is an essential part of the design process , separation of the guidance documents in this way assists the designer and building control surveyor in determining recommendations . <p> One area that is gaining in prominence throughout the regulations and is reflected again in Part B , is that of independent schemes of certification and accreditation . <p> The building industry relies on certification @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ Such properly accredited conformity and certification schemes can provide confidence in quality and performance . While building control may be authorised to accept third party accreditation of installers or systems , designers and developers would be well advised to discuss such issues at an early stage in order that the adequacy of any scheme may be established for Building Regulation purposes . <h> Part C <p> Site preparation and resistance to contaminants and moisture . Many subjects are embraced by Part C. These include the weather and water tightness of buildings ; the prevention of condensation ; subsoil drainage ; site preparation ; and measures to deal with contaminated land , Radon , Methane , and all other site related hazardous and dangerous substances . <h> Part D <p> Toxic substances . This controls against the use of certain toxic materials being used in buildings and this Approved Document was introduced with the Building Regulations 1991 . <h> Part E <p> Resistance to the passage of sound . Requirements for sound insulation between buildings are contained in this part in respect of all new dwellings and the conversion of buildings to @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ came into force on 1 July 2003 . Sound insulation testing generally came into force on 1 July 2003 , but did not apply to new build dwelling houses or flats until 1 July 2004 . Also , as from 1 July 2004 the use of designated robust details , built via the Robust Details Ltd scheme rules , has been allowable for new build dwellings as an alternative to pre-completion sound testing . <h> Part F <p> Ventilation . This deals with ensuring there is adequate ventilation in all types of buildings , both domestic and commercial . <h> Part G <p> Sanitation , hot water safety and water efficiency . Although originally intended to come into effect in October 2009 , a substantially revised Part G incorporating additional requirements for water efficiency and hot water safety came into effect 6 April 2010 . Three new sections have been added : Cold Water Services , Water Efficiency and Food Preparation areas . <p> The principal regulations were amended to require all new dwellings to meet a target efficiency of 125 litres per person per day and notice of the calculation @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ completion certificate can be issued . <h> Part H <p> Drainage and waste disposal . This deals with foul and surface water drainage from buildings , how this is disposed whether by way of sewers or sewerage treatment plants and the storing of solid waste . <h> Part J <p> Combustion appliances and fuel storage systems . These regulations cover the construction , installation and use of boilers , chimneys , flues , hearths and fuel storage installations . They control against fire sources , burning , pollution , and carbon monoxide poisoning . <h> Part K <p> Protection from falling , collision and impact . This sets standards for the safety of stairways , ramps and ladders , together with requirements for balustrades , windows , and vehicle barriers to prevent falling . Also included are requirements for the guarding and warning of hazards through the use and position of doors and windows . <h> Part L <p> Conservation of fuel and power . This part requires a carbon emission rating to be produced for the building as designed and again on completion . From April 2008 it has been @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ Certificate ( EPC ) . There is a need to show that the EPC has been produced by an Accredited Energy Assessor . Revised Approved Documents to Part L came into effect 1 October 2010 . <p> Further changes will be introduced in 2013 , with the aim of achieving a zero carbon emission target by 2016 for dwellings and 2019 for buildings other than dwellings . <h> Part M <p> Access to and use of buildings . This requires the inclusive provision of ease of access to and circulation within all buildings , together with requirements for facilities for disabled people . <h> Part N <p> Glazing -- safety in relation to impact , opening and cleaning . This covers requirements for the use of safety glazing to avoid impact hazard and for the suitable awareness and definition of glazed areas . Also included are safety requirements relating to the use , operation , and cleaning of windows . <h> Part P <p> Electrical safety . This is the newest part of the regulations and came into force in 2006 . It covers the safe installation of electrical installations in @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ electrical circuits in existing dwellings . <p> It should be noted the guidance in the documents are not statutory requirements and do not have to be followed . If you wish to design and construct your building work in some other way , you may do so providing you can show that it still complies with the relevant requirements of the regulations . The guidance in the Approved Documents will be taken into account when considering whether your plans of proposed work , or work in progress , comply with particular requirements . In addition , there is a legal presumption that if you have followed the guidance in the documents , this is evidence that your work has complied with the Building Regulations . <p> Approved Documents can be viewed in full and downloaded here . <h> What work is exempt from the requirements ? <p> A number of minor buildings are exempt from the Building Regulations . These are summarised below . Full details and conditions of exemption are in Schedule 2 of the Building Regulations . It is recommended that detailed checks be made through your local building @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ . <p> Detached buildings not normally used by people <p> Agricultural buildings <p> Greenhouses <p> Buildings for the keeping of animals <p> Temporary buildings in place for less than 28 days <p> Construction site and sales offices <p> Small detached buildings less than 15m ? with no sleeping accommodation <p> Small single storey detached buildings less than 30m ? with no sleeping accommodation and that are more than 1m from boundary or constructed of noncombustible materials <p> Ground level extensions less than 30m ? floor area consisting of a conservatory , covered yard , covered way , porch or a carport open on at least two sides <h> What changes are proposed to the Regulations ? <p> The government is undertaking an in-depth review of the regulations and it is envisaged major changes to some of the regulations will be the outcome of this work . It is believed significant changes will be introduced to the regulations covering conservation of fuel and power to help the government meet its targets in combating climate change and energy issues in general , the likely date for implementing changes being 2013 . Another area @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ were introduced a few years ago and are being reviewed to ascertain if the designed objective of safer electrical installations has been achieved and at a realistic cost . A major exercise was carried out by all Building Control Bodies , public and private , in the early part of 2012 to demonstrate the value and importance of regulations . <p> This was shared with government ministers who were extremely grateful for the work undertaken by building control bodies across the whole of England and Wales . <h> How do I get permission to carry out work ? <p> There are two different routes you can choose to obtain formal consent to carry out your work , and dependant on the scale and type of work involved you have the option of following one of two different procedures : <p> The deposit of a full plans application ; or <p> The giving of a building notice ( except for certain types of building work -- primarily in respect of fire safety issues where a building is used as a workplace or where it may affect a drain ) . <h> Full @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ to contain plans and other information showing all construction details , preferably well in advance of when work is to start on site . Your local authority will check your plans and consult any appropriate authorities ( e.g. fire and sewerage ) . They must complete the procedure by issuing you with a decision within five weeks or , if you agree , a maximum of two months from the date of deposit . <p> If your plans comply with the Building Regulations you will receive a notice stating that they have been approved . If your local authority is not satisfied you may be asked to make amendments or provide more details . Alternatively , a conditional approval may be issued . This will either specify modifications which must be made to the plans ; or will specify further plans which must be deposited with your authority . Your local authority may only apply conditions if you have either requested them to do so or have consented to them doing so . A request or consent must be made in writing . If your plans are rejected the reasons will @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ will carry out inspections of the building work once it is in progress . They will explain about the notification procedures which the regulations require you to follow at various stages of the work -- e.g. in connection with foundations , damp proof courses and drains . In addition , if you request a completion certificate when you first make your application , the local authority will issue it to you provided they are satisfied the completed work complies with the Building Regulations . <p> A further point to bear in mind is that , if a disagreement arises with your local authority , the ' full plans ' procedure enables you to ask for a ' determination ' from ( in England ) the Department for Communities and Local Government or ( in Wales ) the Welsh Assembly Government about whether your plans do or do not comply with the Building Regulations . <h> The building notice procedure <p> This procedure does not involve the passing or rejecting of plans . It therefore avoids the preparation of detailed ' full plans ' , and is designed to enable some types @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ it is perhaps best suited to small work . There are also specific exclusions in the regulations as to when building notices can not be used . These are for building work which is subject to section 1 of the Fire Precautions Act 1971 or Part II of the Fire Precautions ( Workplace ) Regulations 1997 ; for work which will be built close to or over the top of rain water and foul drains shown on the ' map of sewers ' ; and where a new building will front onto a private street . If you decide to use this procedure you need to be confident that the work will comply with the Building Regulations or you will risk having to correct any work you carry out if your local authority requests this . In this respect you do not have the protection provided by the approval of ' full plans ' . <p> Once you have given your ' building notice ' and informed your local authority that you are about to start work , the work will be inspected as it progresses . You will be advised @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ the Building Regulations . If before the start of work , or while work is in progress , your local authority requires further information such as structural design calculations or plans , you must supply the details requested . <p> A local authority is not required to issue a completion certificate under the ' building notice ' procedure ; and because no ' full plans ' are produced it is not possible to ask for a determination if your local authority says your work does not comply with the Building Regulations . <h> Carrying out the work <p> Building control surveyors will inspect the work at pre-designated stages and other crucial stages of the work . Anyone carrying out the work should always give notice of commencement and liaise with the local authority to ensure all key stages of the work are properly inspected and meet with the contractors programme . <h> Completion <p> Upon completion , a final inspection will be carried out by the surveyor and when everything is found to be satisfactory , the local authority will issue a Completion Certificate that indicates compliance and clearance of the @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ introduction of Home Information Packs and requirements for Energy Performance Certificates . <p> This article first appeared in National Directory of Planning , Building Control and Environmental Services 2012 . His book , Chasing Rainbows , on the economics of climate change , is available at Amazon . <h> The wild and wacky world of Willy Hutton <p> Whether the original question " Mummy , what is that man for ? " was about Lord Randolph Churchill or Lord Mounbatten may never be known . But it is the appropriate question to ask about Will Hutton , he who would tell how the economy should and must be run . <p> This weekend we were told that the Government must guarantee , then securitise and sell into the Bank of England , loans made by banks to companies : <p> To do this , the Treasury must indemnify part of new business lending , which is then aggregated into securities that the Bank of England buys . The Americans , Japanese and Germans invented variants of this . So must Britain . <p> What could go @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ , of course ? That child -- say my 12-year-old niece -- would be able to point out that the guarantees are n't going to get put on the good loans to good companies . It 's going to be the dodgy ones that the banks offer up to such a scheme , and thus the dodgy loans that will end up with the BoE . For why would a bank sell off what it thought to be a good loan while it 'll be only to happy to sell off a dodgy one that it 's just made a commission on ? <p> In fact , Woolly Hutton has just recreated the incentive structure that crucified the US mortgage market this past decade . Originate the loan , get paid a fee , then pass off the risk to the guarantor . In the mortgage market those guarantors were Fannie Mae and Freddie Mac . <p> But then Oor Wullie has form on this particular matter . Here he is on 22 June 2008 . <p> The US provides the answer . In these conditions , central banks slash interest @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ risk of a credit implosion is vastly higher than an upward wage and price spiral . Also , the US has only been able to avert disaster in its mortgage market via the guarantees offered by two huge public mortgage banks -- Fannie Mae and Freddie Mac -- which directly or indirectly have provided 80 per cent of all new US mortgages over the last six months . Together , they guarantee more than half of the US 's ? 5 trillion of mortgage debt . <p> In the US , a bank has the safety valve of being able to turn to one of the public mortgage banks which will either buy or , in effect , insure the mortgage and so keep mortgages flowing in bad times . Without them , the US would have suffered an even bigger house price crisis . In Britain , our banks and building societies have no such safety valve and are paralysed . Nor is any immediate relief promised by lowering interest rates . Instead , Bank of England governor Mervyn King threatens a rise . <p> We are looking disaster in @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ Freddie Mac must be created now . Legislation to create a Gordon Mac should be introduced before the summer recess . It should be operating by the end of September . <p> That " operating by the end of September " is fun , for on 7 Spetember 2008 the US announced that Fannie Mae and Freddie Mac were bust and were being taken into " conservatorship " : nationalisation of a failed banking structure by any other name : <p> Sept. 7 ( Bloomberg ) -- The U.S. government seized control of Fannie Mae and Freddie Mac after the biggest surge in mortgage defaults in at least three decades threatened to topple the companies making up almost half the U.S. home-loan market . <p> There was , you 'll not be surprised to hear , a certain cost associated with this rescue : <p> The Congressional Budget Office ( CBO ) says the real cost of the federal government guaranteeing the business of failed mortgage giants Fannie Mae and Freddie Mac is $317 billion <p> Ouch , eh ? <p> To advocate the Fannie/Freddie model before we find out that @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ advocating it after we 've seen them go bust is really quite another . <p> Quite what we should do with a journalist possessing such a track record is a little difficult to say . I would argue that we should try giving him some small , perhaps charitable , organisation to run and see how he does : success at that would at least show that he understood how business and the economy works . Unfortunately , that has already been tried and the Work Foundation went bust within a decade of his taking over . <p> Which leaves us trying to answer that child 's question , " Mummy , what is that man for ? " . The best answer to which is to adapt Fraser Nelson 's comment about La Toynbee : every compass needs its butt end . 
@@884247 <p> Imperial College London today announces plans for new leadership arrangements which will separate the current responsibilities of the Rector , and create a new role of Provost . The search for the first Provost of the College starts today and marks the beginning of the transition towards a leadership model that also includes the new position of President &amp; Rector . <p> Imperial 's Council , the governing body of the College , has agreed to change the current leadership structure for the College . The role of President &amp; Rector will have oversight of all functions at Imperial , but will also place greater emphasis on Imperial 's external affairs in the UK and overseas , focusing on building relationships with alumni , supporters , governments and industry . The Provost will be responsible for advancing and delivering the College 's core academic mission - education , research and translation . <p> Explaining the reasons behind the decision to change the leadership model of the College , Chair of Imperial 's Council , Baroness Manningham-Buller , said : " Higher education is facing tough challenges . @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ the wider world are competing with well-established institutions like ourselves for the best students and staff . We have to be imaginative and flexible both to take advantage of the opportunities we have and to overcome the difficulties we face . " <p> She added , " In changing the leadership structure we can place more emphasis on strengthening our relationships with those who share our vision of the importance of higher education . We need to promote more assiduously Imperial 's reputation , to knit together our extensive network of alumni , to raise funds to secure the future , to cultivate our ability to influence and to work in partnership with governments and other institutions . Doing so will allow Imperial to maintain its position as one of the world 's top universities . " <p> In line with the beginning of the search for a Provost , from today Sir Keith O'Nions assumes the title of President &amp; Rector . Commenting on the new approach , Sir Keith said : <p> " This model is well established in many of the world 's leading universities , most notably @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ the College 's leader , will promote Imperial 's position as a global university , moving it into a different league in terms of development potential , and influence of higher education and research policy . The Provost will ensure the standard and quality of the academic programme is maintained and enhanced . " <p> The post of Provost will report directly to the President &amp; Rector , and together they will have responsibility for the strategic direction of the College . As a member of Council , Imperial College 's highest governing body , the Provost will also share responsibility for the wider governance of the College . <p> At the end of May the search will commence for a new President &amp; Rector to succeed Sir Keith O'Nions following his retirement at the end of 2013 , at which point the new leadership model will be fully in place . <p> Consistently rated amongst the world 's best universities , Imperial College London is a science-based institution with a reputation for excellence in teaching and research that attracts 14,000 students and 6,000 staff of the highest international quality . @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ , medicine , engineering and business , delivering practical solutions that improve quality of life and the environment - underpinned by a dynamic enterprise culture . <p> Since its foundation in 1907 , Imperial 's contributions to society have included the discovery of penicillin , the development of holography and the foundations of fibre optics . This commitment to the application of research for the benefit of all continues today , with current focuses including interdisciplinary collaborations to improve global health , tackle climate change , develop sustainable sources of energy and address security challenges . <p> In 2007 , Imperial College London and Imperial College Healthcare NHS Trust formed the UK 's first Academic Health Science Centre . This unique partnership aims to improve the quality of life of patients and populations by taking new discoveries and translating them into new therapies as quickly as possible . 
@@884435 <h> Homeowners warned the clock is ticking on an insurance trap as floods rise <p> Flooding has hit Britain just weeks after drought warnings were issued . <p> While most homeowners hit this time round should find themselves protected by insurance , the rising waters have served as a reminder that the clock is ticking on a new flood insurance trap . <p> An estimated 200,000 homeowners who live in the highest risk properties could lose their insurance , or see premiums and excesses run into tens of thousands of pounds in just over a year 's time , when a voluntary agreement runs out . <p> We explain the situation and provide a quick guide on what you should do if you are hit by floods . <p> Swollen : Homeowners have been warned of a fresh flood risk after heavy rain . The extent of the downpours can be seen at the swollen River Dee in Llangollen in north Wales <h> What is this flood insurance trap ? <p> A voluntary agreement by insurers to continue offering flood cover will expire in June 2013 and there @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ replace it . <p> Flood cover is provided under the voluntary ' Statement of Principles ' first signed by insurers in 2000 . Under this , insurers will provide cover provided the Government continues to invest in flood defences to protect communities from a one-in-75 years flood . <p> But the deal is far from perfect . Insurers and customers are stuck with each other and there is no incentive for competition in the market . <p> There are no guarantees on how much cover will cost or what will happen to insurance excesses . Properties built since January 2009 are already exempt from the deal . <p> Insurers fear that thanks to the effects of climate change , extreme weather will become more common , and they are reluctant to carry on offering to cover future flood damage . <p> The Association of British Insurers says the agreement was always intended to be nothing more than ' a sticking plaster ' and is negotiating with the Government over what happens from June 2013 . <p> Paul Cobbing , chief executive of the National Flood Forum , a charity that works @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ ' Without insurance you can not recover or reinstate after a flood and may struggle to get a mortgage . People can be stuck with a property they ca n't sell . ' <h> More ... <h> What is being done to fix the problem ? <p> One recent idea is ' Project Noah ' , a scheme that would see insurers pool the potential flood costs for all the homes they insure . They would then ' lay off ' this risk through buying ' catastrophe reinsurance ' in international markets . <p> But the ABI is not keen on Noah , mainly because it relies on global markets to provide the capacity to absorb losses . It argues that neither the price of this reinsurance nor its availability can be guaranteed . Instead , it suggests Government backing for those most at risk of flooding . <p> That would involve a cap on the maximum premium a homeowner would pay . If a property was deemed more risky than this cap , its insurance would transfer into a central fund . <p> Here , the premium paid by the @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ on all British property insurance policies . If there were no big floods in the first two or three years , these annual levies would probably be enough to cover any future losses . <p> However , if there was a flood before the scheme had built up reserves , or there was exceptionally large-scale flooding later on , the Government would stand ready to cover losses . <p> It would effectively grant the flood scheme a loan that could be repaid from future premiums and levies . <p> Discussions continue with the Department for Environment , Food and Rural Affairs , which has promised an announcement before the summer . <h> What is happening to people 's insurance ? <p> Unfortunately for those hit by floods , especially in the latest round of rising waters , insurers are already getting even tougher on flood risk homes . <p> While they must provide flood cover to those homes built before January 2009 , insurers are not bound to keep premiums below a certain level and some householders are finding themselves being quoted huge prices for policies or massive excesses . <p> @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ , they can still insist on a high flood excess -- the portion of a claim the homeowner pays . This can be undertaken without insurer approval ( remember to get receipts ) . <p> There is a range of flood defence products that homeowners can use to protect their property . <p> The British Standards Institution has introduced a kitemark giving consumers reassurance that barriers and flood skirts -- giant rubber sheets that can be lifted up around a house -- are up to scratch .
